I guess... maybe. I just know it's... well, it's what finally pushed me to come in. My shoulders and neck just stay clenched all day. It feels like... I don't know, like I'm bracing for a classroom observation that never ends. And... by the evening, my jaw aches so badly I can hardly even enjoy playing my guitar. So... maybe it's stress. I don't know.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned being fired from the preschool, is that event still causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean... it happened a while ago. So... no. I wouldn't say that.\n\nDoctor: Are your shoulder, neck, and jaw symptoms interfering with your daily routine or activities?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. The tension makes it hard to focus. I've... I've missed some application deadlines for new jobs. And when I do get interviews, I just kind of... stumble through them. I used to volunteer for story-time at the library, but... I stopped doing that unless someone else handles all the planning. So... yeah, I guess it's keeping me from getting back into the classroom.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about things even when there's no apparent reason to worry?\n\nPatient: Um... I suppose so. Yeah. It just... my mind doesn't really stop. I just keep circling back to things. Like... whether I'll ever get hired again, or... if I've somehow ruined my reputation with the parents from before. And then... I start thinking about retirement, and how that feels like it's just slipping away. Even... I don't know, even small things. Like if I make a minor mistake during a story-time activity, I start thinking... maybe I shouldn't be trusted with children anymore. It's just... a lot of circling.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries are worse than what most people would worry about in the same situations?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know. Maybe. I mean... I guess everyone worries about their job and their future. I just... I don't really know how much other people worry about it. It just feels like a lot to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about everyday things, like finances or relationships, even when they're going well?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I haven't... I haven't really noticed that. Things haven't exactly been going well lately, so... I don't really know if I do that or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control your worrying when it starts?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I try to. I tell myself to just... make a list, or pray, or maybe go weed the garden. Just try to focus on the next practical step. But... it doesn't really work. One worry just sort of... hands off to another. And then... I don't know, I realize I've lost half the afternoon to it. So... no.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries about things like your job and reputation are disproportionate to how most people would react in the same situation?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know. I think... I mean, I can't really speak for how other people would react. I don't really talk to anyone else about it, so... I'm not sure if it's out of proportion or not. I just couldn't say.\n\nDoctor: Have your worries been affecting your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I haven't exactly noticed it causing any issues, if that's what you mean. I mostly just... keep to myself anyway. I try not to bother my family with, um, with what's going on in my head. So... I don't think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any social activities or gatherings because of your worries?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think so. I don't really go to a lot of social things to begin with, so... it hasn't really come up. I just go to work and come home, mostly. So... no.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. So... no. I wouldn't say that.\n\nDoctor: Are your shoulder, neck, and jaw symptoms interfering with your daily routine or activities?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. The tension makes it hard to focus. I've... I've missed some application deadlines for new jobs. And when I do get interviews, I just kind of... stumble through them. I used to volunteer for story-time at the library, but... I stopped doing that unless someone else handles all the planning. So... yeah, I guess it's keeping me from getting back into the classroom.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about things even when there's no apparent reason to worry?\n\nPatient: Um... I suppose so. Yeah. It just... my mind doesn't really stop. I just keep circling back to things. Like... whether I'll ever get hired again, or... if I've somehow ruined my reputation with the parents from before. And then... I start thinking about retirement, and how that feels like it's just slipping away. Even... I don't know, even small things. Like if I make a minor mistake during a story-time activity, I start thinking... maybe I shouldn't be trusted with children anymore. It's just... a lot of circling.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries are worse than what most people would worry about in the same situations?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know. Maybe. I mean... I guess everyone worries about their job and their future. I just... I don't really know how much other people worry about it. It just feels like a lot to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about everyday things, like finances or relationships, even when they're going well?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I haven't... I haven't really noticed that. Things haven't exactly been going well lately, so... I don't really know if I do that or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control your worrying when it starts?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I try to. I tell myself to just... make a list, or pray, or maybe go weed the garden. Just try to focus on the next practical step. But... it doesn't really work. One worry just sort of... hands off to another. And then... I don't know, I realize I've lost half the afternoon to it. So... no.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries about things like your job and reputation are disproportionate to how most people would react in the same situation?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know. I think... I mean, I can't really speak for how other people would react. I don't really talk to anyone else about it, so... I'm not sure if it's out of proportion or not. I just couldn't say.\n\nDoctor: Have your worries been affecting your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I haven't exactly noticed it causing any issues, if that's what you mean. I mostly just... keep to myself anyway. I try not to bother my family with, um, with what's going on in my head. So... I don't think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any social activities or gatherings because of your worries?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think so. I don't really go to a lot of social things to begin with, so... it hasn't really come up. I just go to work and come home, mostly. So... no.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your worrying is affecting your ability to perform daily tasks or achieve your goals?\n\nPatient: Um... I suppose it is. I mean... I've missed some application deadlines. And... the interviews I have had, I just kind of stumble through them. I don't know. I'm just here to see about that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this clenching in your shoulders and neck?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's been about a year and two months. That's, uh... that's when I was fired from the preschool. So... since then, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Is the pain in your shoulders, neck, and jaw the main thing that's been bothering you?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I suppose so. It's... it's just the most obvious thing. The aching, I mean. So... yes. That's mainly it.\n\nDoctor: Is the pain in your shoulders, neck, and jaw present all the time?\n\nPatient: Um... pretty much, yeah. It's... it's just kind of always there. Maybe a little worse by the end of the day, but... yeah, mostly all the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the tension in your shoulders, neck, and jaw is related to feeling anxious or stressed?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess... maybe. I just know it's... well, it's what finally pushed me to come in. My shoulders and neck just stay clenched all day. It feels like... I don't know, like I'm bracing for a classroom observation that never ends. And... by the evening, my jaw aches so badly I can hardly even enjoy playing my guitar. So... maybe it's stress. I don't know.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned being fired from the preschool, is that event still causing you significant distress?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean... it happened a while ago. So... no. I wouldn't say that.\n\nDoctor: Are your shoulder, neck, and jaw symptoms interfering with your daily routine or activities?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. The tension makes it hard to focus. I've... I've missed some application deadlines for new jobs. And when I do get interviews, I just kind of... stumble through them. I used to volunteer for story-time at the library, but... I stopped doing that unless someone else handles all the planning. So... yeah, I guess it's keeping me from getting back into the classroom.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about things even when there's no apparent reason to worry?\n\nPatient: Um... I suppose so. Yeah. It just... my mind doesn't really stop. I just keep circling back to things. Like... whether I'll ever get hired again, or... if I've somehow ruined my reputation with the parents from before. And then... I start thinking about retirement, and how that feels like it's just slipping away. Even... I don't know, even small things. Like if I make a minor mistake during a story-time activity, I start thinking... maybe I shouldn't be trusted with children anymore. It's just... a lot of circling.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries are worse than what most people would worry about in the same situations?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know. Maybe. I mean... I guess everyone worries about their job and their future. I just... I don't really know how much other people worry about it. It just feels like a lot to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about everyday things, like finances or relationships, even when they're going well?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I haven't... I haven't really noticed that. Things haven't exactly been going well lately, so... I don't really know if I do that or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can control your worrying when it starts?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I try to. I tell myself to just... make a list, or pray, or maybe go weed the garden. Just try to focus on the next practical step. But... it doesn't really work. One worry just sort of... hands off to another. And then... I don't know, I realize I've lost half the afternoon to it. So... no.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worries about things like your job and reputation are disproportionate to how most people would react in the same situation?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know. I think... I mean, I can't really speak for how other people would react. I don't really talk to anyone else about it, so... I'm not sure if it's out of proportion or not. I just couldn't say.\n\nDoctor: Have your worries been affecting your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I haven't exactly noticed it causing any issues, if that's what you mean. I mostly just... keep to myself anyway. I try not to bother my family with, um, with what's going on in my head. So... I don't think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any social activities or gatherings because of your worries?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't think so. I don't really go to a lot of social things to begin with, so... it hasn't really come up. I just go to work and come home, mostly. So... no.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your worrying is affecting your ability to perform daily tasks or achieve your goals?\n\nPatient: Um... I suppose it is. I mean... I've missed some application deadlines. And... the interviews I have had, I just kind of stumble through them. I don't know. I even stopped volunteering for story-time... well, unless someone else handles all the planning. It's just... the worrying is keeping me from getting back into the classroom in any reliable way. So... yeah. I guess it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is making it hard for you to enjoy things you used to like or be around people you care about?\n\nPatient: Um... not really. I mean... I still like the things I usually do. Mostly just... quiet things at home. And I already mentioned I kind of keep to myself anyway, so... it's not that I can't be around people. I just... I don't think it's affecting that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the tension in your shoulders, neck, and jaw would go away if you weren't worried about things?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't know. I guess... maybe? I haven't really thought about it like that. It's just... always there, so I'm not sure what it would feel like without it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying is causing you a lot of emotional pain or distress?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. It's... it's just hard. I don't really know how to describe it, but... yeah, it's pretty overwhelming sometimes. Just... constantly thinking about everything.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still worry about things even if all the problems in your life were solved?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't really know. I mean... I've never really had a time where absolutely everything was solved, so... it's hard to say. I guess I'm just not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
